45. ACCEPTANCE TEST SCENARIOS

45.0001 Scenario 1: A user asks for a simple explanation and receives a
direct response without unnecessary tool use. 45.0002 Scenario 2: A user
asks for current information and the orchestrator invokes a configured
research/search connector. 45.0003 Scenario 3: A user asks to build a
website and receives a project workspace with preview and source
artifacts. 45.0004 Scenario 4: A user asks to modify one component and
only the requested component changes. 45.0005 Scenario 5: A user asks to
deploy and the system requests approval if deployment approval is
enabled. 45.0006 Scenario 6: A user asks to write code and the system
can create files inside the selected project. 45.0007 Scenario 7: A user
asks to run tests and the system executes them in an isolated
environment. 45.0008 Scenario 8: A test fails and the coding agent can
inspect the failure and propose a patch. 45.0009 Scenario 9: A user asks
for an image and receives an actual image artifact when a provider is
connected. 45.0010 Scenario 10: A user asks for a video and receives a
durable generation job rather than a fake success message. 45.0011
Scenario 11: A user asks for a presenter video and the avatar provider
is used only if configured. 45.0012 Scenario 12: A user uploads a
document and the assistant can retrieve relevant content within
authorized scope. 45.0013 Scenario 13: A user asks for data analysis and
the source dataset remains unchanged unless mutation is explicitly
requested. 45.0014 Scenario 14: A user creates a workflow and can
dry-run it before activation. 45.0015 Scenario 15: A workflow requiring
approval pauses at the approval node. 45.0016 Scenario 16: A browser
agent reaches a purchase step and pauses when purchase approval is
required. 45.0017 Scenario 17: A provider fails and a compatible
fallback is attempted when configured. 45.0018 Scenario 18: All provider
failures are represented honestly when no fallback succeeds. 45.0019
Scenario 19: A user refreshes the browser during a long-running job and
the job remains visible. 45.0020 Scenario 20: An administrator disables
a model and new requests stop routing to it. 45.0021 Scenario 21: A
normal user attempts an admin URL and receives an authorization failure.
45.0022 Scenario 22: A user cannot retrieve another user’s project
files. 45.0023 Scenario 23: A user cannot read another workspace’s
secrets. 45.0024 Scenario 24: A deleted resource is no longer accessible
according to retention policy. 45.0025 Scenario 25: A billing webhook is
delivered twice and produces one logical ledger change. 45.0026 Scenario
26: A tool timeout produces a recoverable error state. 45.0027 Scenario
27: A canceled generation does not later overwrite the canceled state
without a valid provider completion event. 45.0028 Scenario 28: A shared
link respects its configured visibility. 45.0029 Scenario 29: A mobile
user can approve a pending action without opening desktop. 45.0030
Scenario 30: A user can move from chat to a generated artifact and back
without losing context. 45.0031 Scenario 31: A user asks for a simple
explanation and receives a direct response without unnecessary tool use.

================================================================================================PART


================================================================================
PART 8: KYMEX AI — ADMIN CONSOLE IMPLEMENTATION SPECIFICATION
================================================================================

SCOPE LOCK

The following 25 sections are ADMIN CONSOLE ONLY. They are not ordinary
end-user application pages and must not be placed in the normal user
sidebar. The Admin Console is a separate privileged management surface
with server-enforced authorization.

The end-user application remains focused on user-facing AI, chat,
projects, conversations, personal files, permitted agents, permitted
integrations, and other capabilities explicitly designated as
user-facing elsewhere in the specification.

The Admin Console manages platform configuration, organization-wide
resources, administrative operations, security, financial controls,
integrations, deployment infrastructure, analytics, and auditability.

This section replaces the ambiguous “Application Control Center /
Build-Console” interpretation from earlier expansion material. It does
not remove any underlying capability; it establishes the correct
administrative scope for these management pages.

ADMIN CONSOLE MASTER REQUIREMENTS

1.  Render the Admin Console behind a dedicated administrative route
    namespace.
2.  Enforce authorization on every server request; hiding navigation is
    never a security boundary.
3.  Require an authenticated identity before any administrative data is
    returned.
4.  Evaluate organization, workspace, tenant, role, permission,
    environment, and resource scope independently.
5.  Return authorization failures without leaking protected resource
    metadata.
6.  Keep administrative APIs separate from ordinary user APIs where
    practical.
7.  Apply least privilege to every administrative permission.
8.  Record security-sensitive administrative mutations in the Activity
    Log.
9.  Require step-up authentication for actions classified as high
    impact.
10. Support explicit confirmation for destructive or financially
    consequential operations.
11. Show current environment and tenant context prominently.
12. Never silently switch a selected organization or environment during
    an operation.
13. Preserve unsaved form state only when doing so does not retain
    secrets insecurely.
14. Never expose raw secrets in ordinary browser state, analytics
    events, error traces, or AI context.
15. Display truthful operation states: queued, running, succeeded,
    partially succeeded, failed, canceled, blocked, or awaiting
    approval.
16. Use idempotency keys for retryable administrative mutations.
17. Give long-running operations an operation identifier and status
    endpoint.
18. Verify backend state before showing a mutation as completed.
19. Make every page usable on desktop and mobile without weakening
    security controls.
20. Provide accessible keyboard navigation, focus management, labels,
    and error announcements.
21. Keep administrative UI visually consistent with KYMEX while clearly
    signaling privileged context.
22. Do not expose platform-wide data to users merely because an
    administrator can see it.
23. Separate global configuration from per-user configuration.
24. Support audit-friendly before/after representations for mutable
    settings where safe.
25. Prevent prompt injection from external data from changing
    administrative authorization decisions.
26. Require explicit policy checks before invoking custom code, external
    tools, or deployment actions.
27. Treat provider availability as dynamic; never assume an integration
    exists merely because its name appears in documentation.
28. Use capability discovery rather than hard-coded assumptions when an
    external service changes its tool set.
29. Keep configuration changes versioned where rollback is technically
    feasible.
30. Make destructive actions recoverable whenever the underlying
    provider supports recovery.
31. Provide consistent loading, empty, success, error, and
    permission-denied states.
32. Include contextual documentation without replacing operational
    controls with static instructions.
33. Ensure every form has validation before submission and server
    validation after submission.
34. Prevent double-submission by disabling or deduplicating mutations
    while a request is active.
35. Show validation errors adjacent to affected fields and summarize
    them for accessibility.
36. Preserve audit correlation IDs across UI, API, worker, and provider
    boundaries.
37. Use structured telemetry that excludes credentials and sensitive
    payload contents.
38. Respect data retention and privacy rules when storing administrative
    telemetry.
39. Allow authorized administrators to export permitted operational data
    without bypassing access policies.
40. Ensure exports themselves receive access controls and audit records.
41. Keep user-level permissions intact when an administrator configures
    a global connector.
42. Distinguish configuration availability from actual runtime
    authorization.
43. Distinguish provider connection health from user entitlement.
44. Distinguish successful API acknowledgement from verified resource
    state.
45. Never fabricate counts, balances, traffic, subscriptions, tool
    availability, or deployment status.
46. Make feature flags explicit and environment-aware.
47. Protect production configuration from accidental edits made in
    development contexts.
48. Require deliberate environment selection before production-impacting
    actions.
49. Provide safe defaults for newly created administrative resources.
50. Preserve all existing KYMEX AI user-facing functionality while
    implementing this console.

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 01: OVERVIEW
================================================================================

PURPOSE

The Overview page is an administrative surface for platform health,
deployments, users, environments, jobs, incidents, capacity, feature
flags, and operational summaries. It must operate within the selected
tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and must never become a
normal end-user navigation destination.

ROUTE AND PERMISSION CONTRACT

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/overview. The server
must require the dedicated permission for Overview before returning page
data. The permission check must be repeated by every mutation endpoint
used by the page. A frontend route guard is useful for UX but is never
sufficient for authorization.

PAGE-SPECIFIC REQUIREMENTS

01.001 Overview requirement 1: Support scoped search using normalized
names, identifiers, status values, and relevant metadata. For this page,
apply that rule specifically to platform health, deployments, users,
environments, jobs, incidents, capacity, feature flags, and operational
summaries, using the selected overview context where applicable. 01.002
Overview requirement 2: Allow filtering by environment and tenant
without changing the authorization context implicitly. For this page,
apply that rule specifically to platform health, deployments, users,
environments, jobs, incidents, capacity, feature flags, and operational
summaries, using the selected overview context where applicable. 01.003
Overview requirement 3: Provide a clear empty state explaining what
creates the first resource without pretending that sample data is live.
For this page, apply that rule specifically to platform health,
deployments, users, environments, jobs, incidents, capacity, feature

where applicable. 01.004 Overview requirement 4: Expose

overview context where applicable. 01.005 Overview requirement 5: Use

01.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/overview must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

01.DATA DATA MODEL

Store only the fields required for operational behavior, auditing,
retention, and support. Every mutable record should have a stable
identifier, tenant or organization scope, environment where relevant,
created timestamp, updated timestamp, actor metadata where appropriate,
and a version or concurrency token when edits can collide. Relationships
must reference stable identifiers rather than display names.

01.SEC SECURITY MODEL

Treat this page as privileged. Enforce role permissions at the service
layer, apply tenant isolation, validate resource ownership, protect
secrets, sanitize provider output before rendering, and prevent
untrusted external text from becoming administrative instructions.
High-impact actions must pass the central Action Authorization Engine.

01.UX STATES

Provide first-load skeletons, permission-denied states, genuinely empty
states, recoverable provider-error states, validation feedback, mutation
progress, verified success, partial completion, and explicit
uncertainty. Never replace an error with fabricated sample data.

01.AUDIT EVENTS

Define distinct events for viewing sensitive configuration, creating a
resource, modifying configuration, enabling or disabling a capability,
rotating credentials, deleting or purging data, exporting information,
and executing high-impact actions where those operations apply to this
page.

01.TEST MATRIX

Test an authorized read, unauthorized read, authorized mutation,
unauthorized mutation, malformed input, stale version, duplicate
submission, provider timeout, provider rejection, partial failure,
successful verification, browser refresh during mutation, mobile
rendering, keyboard navigation, and audit-event emission.

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 02: BRANDING
================================================================================

The Branding page is an administrative surface for logos, icons,
application name, typography, color tokens, themes, email identity,
metadata, and brand assets. It must operate within the selected tenant,
workspace, environment, and role scope and must never become a normal
end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/branding. The server
must require the dedicated permission for Branding before returning page
data. The permission check must be repeated by every mutation endpoint
used by the page. A frontend route guard is useful for UX but is never
sufficient for authorization.

02.001 Branding requirement 1: Expose provider-specific fields only
after capability discovery confirms that the provider supports them. For
this page, apply that rule specifically to logos, icons, application
name, typography, color tokens, themes, email identity, metadata, and
brand assets, using the selected branding context where applicable.
02.002 Branding requirement 2: Use optimistic UI only for reversible
presentation changes; authoritative backend state remains the source of

02.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/branding must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

02.SEC SECURITY MODEL

02.AUDIT EVENTS

02.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 03: INSTALL AS APP
================================================================================

The Install as app page is an administrative surface for PWA metadata,
install prompts, service workers, manifests, mobile behavior, update
channels, and platform installation settings. It must operate within the
selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and must never
become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/install-as-app. The
server must require the dedicated permission for Install as app before
returning page data. The permission check must be repeated by every
mutation endpoint used by the page. A frontend route guard is useful for
UX but is never sufficient for authorization.

03.001 Install as app requirement 1: Make asynchronous work resumable
where supported and expose progress without exposing internal
credentials. For this page, apply that rule specifically to PWA
metadata, install prompts, service workers, manifests, mobile behavior,
update channels, and platform installation settings, using the selected
install as app context where applicable. 03.002 Install as app
requirement 2: Attach an operation ID to meaningful mutations so support
staff can correlate UI and backend records. For this page, apply that
rule specifically to PWA metadata, install prompts, service workers,
manifests, mobile behavior, update channels, and platform installation
settings, using the selected install as app context where applicable.
03.003 Install as app requirement 3: Record actor, target, action,
result, timestamp, environment, and correlation identifier for auditable
mutations. For this page, apply that rule specifically to PWA metadata,
install prompts, service workers, manifests, mobile behavior, update
channels, and platform installation settings, using the selected install
as app context where applicable. 03.004 Install as app requirement 4:
Require confirmation text or an explicit secondary confirmation for
irreversible operations when appropriate. For this page, apply that rule
specifically to PWA metadata, install prompts, service workers,
manifests, mobile behavior, update channels, and platform installation
settings, using the selected install as app context where applicable.
03.005 Install as app requirement 5: Offer a safe preview or diff for
configuration changes that alter runtime behavior. For this page, apply
that rule specifically to PWA metadata, install prompts, service

installation settings, using the selected install as app context where
applicable. 03.006 Install as app requirement 6: Validate referenced

03.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/install-as-app must return only fields permitted by the
caller’s administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose
pagination metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request
against the latest resource version and must return an operation
identifier when work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s
standard error envelope and must not expose provider credentials or
protected implementation details.

03.DATA DATA MODEL

03.SEC SECURITY MODEL

03.AUDIT EVENTS

03.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 04: DATABASE
================================================================================

The Database page is an administrative surface for schemas, tables,
indexes, migrations, records, backups, query tools, retention, access
policies, and database health. It must operate within the selected
tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and must never become a
normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/database. The server
must require the dedicated permission for Database before returning page
data. The permission check must be repeated by every mutation endpoint
used by the page. A frontend route guard is useful for UX but is never
sufficient for authorization.

04.001 Database requirement 1: Require confirmation text or an explicit
secondary confirmation for irreversible operations when appropriate. For
this page, apply that rule specifically to schemas, tables, indexes,
migrations, records, backups, query tools, retention, access policies,
and database health, using the selected database context where
applicable. 04.002 Database requirement 2: Offer a safe preview or diff
for configuration changes that alter runtime behavior. For this page,
apply that rule specifically to schemas, tables, indexes, migrations,
records, backups, query tools, retention, access policies, and database
health, using the selected database context where applicable. 04.003
Database requirement 3: Validate referenced resources before saving
relationships so broken dependencies cannot be silently stored. For this
page, apply that rule specifically to schemas, tables, indexes,
migrations, records, backups, query tools, retention, access policies,
and database health, using the selected database context where
applicable. 04.004 Database requirement 4: Use transactional writes when
multiple records must change together; otherwise expose partial-failure
behavior honestly. For this page, apply that rule specifically to

database context where applicable. 04.005 Database requirement 5:
Provide retry controls only for operations known to be safe to retry or
protected by idempotency. For this page, apply that rule specifically to

04.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/database must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

04.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 05: FILES
================================================================================

The Files page is an administrative surface for global file inventory,
quarantine, ownership, permissions, previews, version history,
retention, scanning, and administrative file actions. It must operate
within the selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and
must never become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/files. The server must
require the dedicated permission for Files before returning page data.
The permission check must be repeated by every mutation endpoint used by
the page. A frontend route guard is useful for UX but is never
sufficient for authorization.

05.001 Files requirement 1: Use transactional writes when multiple
records must change together; otherwise expose partial-failure behavior
honestly. For this page, apply that rule specifically to global file
inventory, quarantine, ownership, permissions, previews, version
history, retention, scanning, and administrative file actions, using the
selected files context where applicable. 05.002 Files requirement 2:
Provide retry controls only for operations known to be safe to retry or
protected by idempotency. For this page, apply that rule specifically to
global file inventory, quarantine, ownership, permissions, previews,
version history, retention, scanning, and administrative file actions,
using the selected files context where applicable. 05.003 Files
requirement 3: Expose health diagnostics separately from remediation
actions so observing a fault cannot accidentally mutate it. For this
page, apply that rule specifically to global file inventory, quarantine,
ownership, permissions, previews, version history, retention, scanning,
and administrative file actions, using the selected files context where
applicable. 05.004 Files requirement 4: Keep sensitive values masked and
provide rotation or replacement flows rather than routine plaintext
viewing. For this page, apply that rule specifically to global file
inventory, quarantine, ownership, permissions, previews, version
history, retention, scanning, and administrative file actions, using the
selected files context where applicable. 05.005 Files requirement 5:

specifically to global file inventory, quarantine, ownership,

05.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/files must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

05.SEC SECURITY MODEL

05.UX STATES

05.AUDIT EVENTS

05.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 06: STORAGE
================================================================================

The Storage page is an administrative surface for buckets, volumes,
quotas, lifecycle policies, replication, object metadata, cleanup,
capacity alerts, and provider configuration. It must operate within the
selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and must never
become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/storage. The server
must require the dedicated permission for Storage before returning page
data. The permission check must be repeated by every mutation endpoint
used by the page. A frontend route guard is useful for UX but is never
sufficient for authorization.

06.001 Storage requirement 1: Keep sensitive values masked and provide
rotation or replacement flows rather than routine plaintext viewing. For
this page, apply that rule specifically to buckets, volumes, quotas,
lifecycle policies, replication, object metadata, cleanup, capacity
alerts, and provider configuration, using the selected storage context
where applicable. 06.002 Storage requirement 2: Respect environment
boundaries when cloning configuration and require explicit destination
confirmation. For this page, apply that rule specifically to buckets,
volumes, quotas, lifecycle policies, replication, object metadata,
cleanup, capacity alerts, and provider configuration, using the selected
storage context where applicable. 06.003 Storage requirement 3: Support
import/export through validated schemas with size, type, and
authorization checks. For this page, apply that rule specifically to
buckets, volumes, quotas, lifecycle policies, replication, object

the selected storage context where applicable. 06.004 Storage
requirement 4: Prevent unauthorized cross-tenant references when
administrators search globally. For this page, apply that rule

into accessible cards or horizontally scrollable regions without hiding

06.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/storage must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

06.AUDIT EVENTS

06.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 07: ADD-ONS
================================================================================

The Add-ons page is an administrative surface for feature packages,
extensions, entitlement rules, versions, compatibility, configuration,
installation, upgrades, and rollback. It must operate within the
selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and must never
become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/add-ons. The server
must require the dedicated permission for Add-ons before returning page
data. The permission check must be repeated by every mutation endpoint
used by the page. A frontend route guard is useful for UX but is never
sufficient for authorization.

07.001 Add-ons requirement 1: Prevent unauthorized cross-tenant
references when administrators search globally. For this page, apply
that rule specifically to feature packages, extensions, entitlement
rules, versions, compatibility, configuration, installation, upgrades,
and rollback, using the selected add-ons context where applicable.

07.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/add-ons must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

07.SEC SECURITY MODEL

07.UX STATES

07.AUDIT EVENTS

07.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 08: BOTS & AGENTS
================================================================================

The Bots & Agents page is an administrative surface for global agents,
bot templates, tools, policies, versions, runtime settings, deployment
state, permissions, and agent governance. It must operate within the
selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and must never
become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/bots-and-agents. The
server must require the dedicated permission for Bots & Agents before
returning page data. The permission check must be repeated by every
mutation endpoint used by the page. A frontend route guard is useful for
UX but is never sufficient for authorization.

08.001 Bots & Agents requirement 1: Expose machine-readable error codes
internally while presenting actionable human-readable messages in the
UI. For this page, apply that rule specifically to global agents, bot
templates, tools, policies, versions, runtime settings, deployment
state, permissions, and agent governance, using the selected bots &
agents context where applicable. 08.002 Bots & Agents requirement 2:
Avoid revealing whether a protected resource exists when the viewer
lacks permission to enumerate it. For this page, apply that rule
specifically to global agents, bot templates, tools, policies, versions,
runtime settings, deployment state, permissions, and agent governance,
using the selected bots & agents context where applicable. 08.003 Bots &
Agents requirement 3: Cache non-sensitive metadata with explicit
invalidation rules so administrative dashboards remain fast without

settings, deployment state, permissions, and agent governance, using the
selected bots & agents context where applicable. 08.004 Bots & Agents

imports, exports, migrations, and analytics calculations. For this page,
apply that rule specifically to global agents, bot templates, tools,
policies, versions, runtime settings, deployment state, permissions, and
agent governance, using the selected bots & agents context where
applicable. 08.005 Bots & Agents requirement 5: Show worker queue status
and last successful execution where that information is safe and

08.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/bots-and-agents must return only fields permitted by the
caller’s administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose
pagination metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request
against the latest resource version and must return an operation
identifier when work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s
standard error envelope and must not expose provider credentials or
protected implementation details.

08.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 09: WORKFLOWS
================================================================================

The Workflows page is an administrative surface for automation
definitions, triggers, schedules, nodes, dependencies, execution
history, retries, approvals, and workflow governance. It must operate
within the selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and
must never become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/workflows. The server
must require the dedicated permission for Workflows before returning
page data. The permission check must be repeated by every mutation
endpoint used by the page. A frontend route guard is useful for UX but
is never sufficient for authorization.

09.001 Workflows requirement 1: Use background workers for expensive
scans, rebuilds, imports, exports, migrations, and analytics
calculations. For this page, apply that rule specifically to automation
definitions, triggers, schedules, nodes, dependencies, execution
history, retries, approvals, and workflow governance, using the selected
workflows context where applicable. 09.002 Workflows requirement 2: Show
worker queue status and last successful execution where that information
is safe and relevant. For this page, apply that rule specifically to
automation definitions, triggers, schedules, nodes, dependencies,
execution history, retries, approvals, and workflow governance, using
the selected workflows context where applicable. 09.003 Workflows
requirement 3: Prevent browser refresh or navigation from accidentally
repeating a privileged mutation. For this page, apply that rule
specifically to automation definitions, triggers, schedules, nodes,
dependencies, execution history, retries, approvals, and workflow
governance, using the selected workflows context where applicable.
09.004 Workflows requirement 4: Require current-context verification
immediately before high-impact operations whose target may have changed.
For this page, apply that rule specifically to automation definitions,
triggers, schedules, nodes, dependencies, execution history, retries,
approvals, and workflow governance, using the selected workflows context
where applicable. 09.005 Workflows requirement 5: Support notification

09.API API CONTRACT

GET /admin/workflows must return only fields permitted by the caller’s
administrative scope. Collection endpoints must expose pagination
metadata. Mutation endpoints must validate the request against the
latest resource version and must return an operation identifier when
work continues asynchronously. Errors must use KYMEX’s standard error
envelope and must not expose provider credentials or protected
implementation details.

09.DATA DATA MODEL

09.SEC SECURITY MODEL

09.UX STATES

09.AUDIT EVENTS

09.TEST MATRIX

================================================================================
ADMIN CONSOLE PAGE 10: VOICE
================================================================================

The Voice page is an administrative surface for voice providers, speech
settings, voices, languages, limits, routing, transcription, synthesis,
quality controls, and voice governance. It must operate within the
selected tenant, workspace, environment, and role scope and must never
become a normal end-user navigation destination.

Use an implementation route equivalent to /admin/voice. The server must
require the dedicated permission for Voice before returning page data.
The permission check must be repeated by every mutation endpoint used by
the page. A frontend route guard is useful for UX but is never
sufficient for authorization.

10.001 Voice requirement 1: Require current-context verification
immediately before high-impact operations whose target may have changed.
For this page, apply that rule specifically to voice providers, speech
settings, voices, languages, limits, routing, transcription, synthesis,
quality controls, and voice governance, using the selected voice context
where applicable. 10.002 Voice requirement 2: Support notification hooks
for failures without putting secrets or sensitive payloads into
notification content. For this page, apply that rule specifically to
voice providers, speech settings, voices, languages, limits, routing,
transcription, synthesis, quality controls, and voice governance, using
the selected voice context where applicable. 10.003 Voice requirement 3:
Make configuration history searchable when versioning is enabled and
show which actor introduced each version. For this page, apply that rule
